Thus the structure used in the rare-token theorem can follow from observation on raw words, under a finite identification assumption.

**Finite certificates.** A shortest word reaching a state has length at most $q-1$. If a nonconstant output realizes $h$ distinct values, two distinguishable states have a separating suffix of length at most $q-h$: partition initially by current outputs and refine by the tuple of successor classes until stable. Each nonterminal refinement increases the number of classes. Thus either individual action law, if false, has a witness pair with longest word of length at most $2q-1$ for a binary nonconstant readout. Without a justified upper state bound this reasoning cannot certify unobserved continuations. Conversely, noiseless short-word rows with suffixes through $q-2$ distinguish the states; extending shortest representatives by one letter constructs their transitions from observable rows.

Testing *both* laws together can use shorter words when the alphabet is small. Define a candidate by reading $g$ on the canonical sorted words with each odd letter once. Its distinct states are distinct translates of $g$. If two such translates differ, their disagreement set contains at least two vectors: translation by their nonzero difference exchanges the values at each disagreeing vector and its paired vector. Hence canonical suffixes of length at most $d-1$ separate all distinct candidate states, since they omit only the all-ones parity. Choose any shortest access word $u_s$ for each actual state $s$. If the candidate and actual law agree on all words through $q+d-1$, their rows after $u_sa$ and after $u_{T_a s}$ give the same answers to the separating suffixes. The corresponding candidate states are equal. The map from actual states to the candidate states reached by their access words thus preserves every transition, the initial state and the readout, so all words agree. In particular, horizon $q+1$ suffices for joint binary-letter certification. Rewriting any disagreement word to canonical form via swaps and deletions produces a single observable failed identity within the same horizon. This is an upper bound on maximal word length, distinct from the number of words needed.

**The price of universal certification is exponential.** For two letters and a supplied state upper bound $Q\ge4$, the worst-case number of noiseless word probes for testing both laws at fixed confidence is $\Theta(2^Q)$. Querying every word through $Q+1$ attains the upper bound. For the lower bound take the nonconstant two-state parity character counting occurrences of the first letter. Flip its answer on exactly one unknown binary word $w$ of length $L=Q-3$. This needs exactly $L+3$ minimal states: keep one state for each matching prefix length $0,\ldots,L$, then either of the two base parity states after divergence. Prefix states with a different underlying parity separate on every sufficiently long common continuation, and those with the same parity separate by completing one matching prefix to $w$. The two base states are reachable. The changed word and that word followed by two equal letters violate square deletion. A tester using the same coins on the baseline and the defective source sees identical answers until it submits $w$. If its two errors are both at most $\delta$, it must query $w$ with baseline probability at least $1-2\delta$. Summing over $2^L$ possible words gives the lower bound even for adaptive expected work. With no state ceiling at all, any finite accepting baseline transcript misses all but finitely many isolated defects. Along distinct growing-length defects, the probability of accepting and missing tends to the baseline acceptance probability by bounded convergence. No finite acceptance protocol can then have both errors uniformly below $1/2$.

Suppose each fresh complete word query independently flips the answer with known fixed rate $0<\eta<1/2$. For $0<\delta\le1/3$ the minimax probe cost is $\Theta_\eta(2^Q\ln(1/\delta))$. First fit the four canonical answers by fresh majorities at error at most $\delta/3$ in total. Conditional on their correctness, each word through $Q+1$ produces a candidate-disagreement bit of mean either $\eta$ or $1-\eta$. To detect whether any high-mean word exists, run a binary elimination tournament on these $K=O(2^Q)$ words. At level $j\ge1$, estimate each remaining word's mean with $O((j+\ln(1/\delta))/(1-2\eta)^2)$ fresh answers, advance the larger estimate in each pair, and use byes when necessary. Track one high word if it exists; if its opponent is also high the winner is high. Against a low opponent the conditional chance of losing is at most $\delta 2^{-j}/3$ by Hoeffding with a sufficiently large fixed constant. Final fresh validation at error $\delta/3$ handles the all-low case independently of the search. The total work sums a geometric sequence in the decreasing number of entrants. Conversely, on the baseline the transcript divergence against each one-word alternative is $\mathbb E_0 N_w (1-2\eta)\ln((1-\eta)/\eta)$, by conditioning on successive word choices. For any stopping scheme with finite expected length this follows by applying the KL chain rule to truncated transcripts and taking the limit. The final binary decision has divergence at least $\mathrm{kl}(1-\delta,\delta)$ by data processing. Summing gives the matching lower bound. If each fresh answer requires replay from reset, the sharp raw-letter price is $\Theta_\eta(Q2^Q\ln(1/\delta))$; without noise it is $\Theta(Q2^Q)$ at fixed confidence. Cached states, repeated reads without replay, equivalence queries and deployment-weighted error are different observation contracts. A defect on one word changes ordinary predictive risk only through that word's probability and conditional label separation. [Unrestricted acquisition on raw letters and complete finite checks](EVIDENCE.md#learning-token-actions-without-supplying-a-toggle-law)

## What a predictive state must forget to keep learning from recent inputs

Let $S_j=T_{X_j}(S_{j-1})$ be a deterministic online update with at most $m$ live states. At a fixed time or publicly known phase define its logical erasure on the realized distribution as $E_j=H(S_{j-1}\mid S_j,X_j)$. This measures the uncertainty about the old register given the new one and the actual raw input. A declared many-to-one map can have zero erasure on a particular data distribution. Any per-input permutation has zero erasure on every distribution. Entropies are in nats. Physical dissipation is a different quantity and no energy conversion is assumed.

**PROVED.** For the length-$k$ input window $W_t=X_{t-k+1:t}$, the earlier state $A=S_{t-k}$ and the current state $S=S_t$,
$$
I(S;W_t)=H(S)-H(A)+I(A;W_t)+H(A\mid S,W_t),
\qquad H(A\mid S,W_t)\le\sum_{j=t-k+1}^t E_j .
$$
Indeed $S$ is a function of $(A,W_t)$, so the chain rule gives the identity. Given the window and the old state all intermediate states are determined. Expanding their conditional entropy in reverse order gives $H(A\mid S,W_t)=\sum_j H(S_{j-1}\mid S_j,W_t)$, since $(S_j,W_t)$ determines all subsequent states. Dropping the other window letters gives the upper bound. Any target $Y_t$ and its best probabilistic readout then satisfy
$$
I(Y_t;S_t)\le H(S_t)-H(S_{t-k})+
I(S_{t-k};W_t,Y_t)+\sum_{j=t-k+1}^t E_j .
$$
To see this, $I(Y;S)\le I(S;W)+I(Y;S\mid W)$ and data processing conditional on $W$ gives $I(Y;S\mid W)\le I(Y;A\mid W)$. The predictive gain here is the population reduction in log loss from the marginal using a calibrated readout; other fitted releases can earn less.

If the raw inputs are independent fresh draws, the initial state is independent of them, and $Y_t$ is generated from its own window with fresh conditional noise independent of the earlier state, then $I(S_{t-k};W_t,Y_t)=0$. For $T\ge k$ it follows that
$$
\sum_{t=k}^T I(Y_t;S_t)\le k\ln m+k\sum_{j=1}^T E_j .
$$
The entropy differences telescope separately along each residue class modulo $k$, and each individual erasure is counted at most $k$ times. For $k=1$ and target $Y_t=X_t$ the single-step identity is an equality, $I(S_t;X_t)=H(S_t)-H(S_{t-1})+E_t$. In particular, any finite machine with per-letter permutations can earn at most $k\ln m$ total nats about fixed-window targets across arbitrarily many fresh steps. Reversible updates can still track a long-range parity exactly. Keeping the most recent of iid fair bits instead needs one live bit and loses $\ln2$ logical nats per overwrite after the initial step, sustaining $\ln2$ nats of predictive gain per copy query.

The factor $k$ on erasure can be attained asymptotically with a known phase. Store one fresh fair bit every $k$ steps, hold it in between, and let the answer at every step request the most recent scheduled bit, which lies in its allowed window. The one-bit update discards $\ln2$ nats per overwrite after the first and the readout earns $\ln2$ nats at every subsequent query. If the phase is internally maintained, charge its states and update. If instead a larger reversible machine logs discarded values or can reread input history, the total accessible store and its reset or reread costs belong to the accounting. Reversible simulation with retained history and uncomputation is a different physical execution model. [Bennett, *Logical Reversibility of Computation*](https://doi.org/10.1147/rd.176.0525)

Independence cannot be weakened to ordinary mixing without an inherited-information term. Let $Z_j$ be independent biased signs of mean $\mu$ with $0<|\mu|<1$, and set $X_j=Z_{j-1}Z_j$. This input is stationary and one-dependent. Starting from $S_0=1$, reversible multiplication by $X_j$ yields $S_t=Z_0Z_t$. For $t\ge2$ the covariance with the most recent input is $\mu^2(1-\mu^2)>0$. The state supplies persistent current-input information without erasure by inheriting correlations across the window boundary. The complete identity, rather than its independent-input corollary, is applicable to natural text. [Held-out word measurements and empirical entropy balances](EVIDENCE.md#which-update-did-ordinary-text-select)

The coefficient $k$ is sharp even for a **stationary homogeneous two-state machine with iid inputs and binary answers**. Give it input letters hold, write-zero and write-one, with a write occurring at independent rate $\lambda>0$ and the written value an independent Bernoulli($\pi$) bit. Initialize the register in its stationary Bernoulli($\pi$) law. Then $E=\lambda h(\pi)$. Let $A$ indicate that at least one write occurred in the last $k$ steps, giving $a=P(A)=1-(1-\lambda)^k$. Conditional on the occurrence pattern the current bit still has law Bernoulli($\pi$), so $A$ and $S$ are independent. Define the ordinary binary target as one exactly when the latest write in the window was a one; no write gives zero. Thus $Y=AS$ and $I(Y;S)=h(a\pi)-\pi h(a)$. As $\pi\downarrow0$ the ratio $I(Y;S)/h(\pi)$ tends to $a$, so $I(Y;S)/E$ tends to $a/\lambda$, which in turn tends to $k$ as $\lambda\downarrow0$. Any smaller universal coefficient would fail after first taking a long stationary run and then choosing the rates. A ternary response revealing a distinct no-write symbol gives $I(Y;S)=a h(\pi)$ exactly.

For a stationary finite machine driven by iid letters, recent-window information also has a decomposition by age. Put $a_{j,t}=I(X_j;S_t\mid X_{j+1:t})$. The reverse chain rule gives $I(S_t;W_t)=\sum_{j=t-k+1}^t a_{j,t}$. Conditioning on a fresh independent next letter and applying its transition cannot increase any earlier contribution, so $a_{j,t+1}\le a_{j,t}$. In stationarity write these as $a_0,a_1,\ldots$. The first contribution is $a_0=E$, subsequent contributions decrease, and their sum is at most $H(S)$. Thus the window information is concave in the integer window length. For a two-state machine, each letter either permutes the two states or sends both to the same state. If the total probability of the latter letters is $\lambda$ and the stationary state law is $\pi$, conditioning on a length-$k$ word leaves entropy $H(\pi)$ exactly if it contains only permutations, and zero otherwise. Hence $I(S_t;W_t)=H(\pi)[1-(1-\lambda)^k]$ and $a_j=\lambda H(\pi)(1-\lambda)^j$ for every $j\ge0$.

There is a general stationary limit. Suppose the finite induced state chain is irreducible, its input letters are fresh iid with positive probability for every supported letter, and it starts in its unique stationary law $\pi$. Let $r_*$ be the smallest image size of any supported transition word. Then
$$
\lim_{k\to\infty} I(S_t;X_{t-k+1:t})=H(\pi)-\ln r_* .
$$
Here the limit uses a stationary process or the time-independent finite-window law. Yano's Theorem 4.1 establishes uniform stationary fiber masses for a minimum-rank word in the road-coloring model under the assumptions of its Theorem 2.8. For this one-stationary-law entropy assertion the following finite proof does not need aperiodicity. Partition the collection of images by their size and retain the set of all images of size $r_*$. A letter sends any such image bijectively onto another one of that size, by minimality of $r_*$. Choose a stationary law of the induced finite chain of these sets, then a uniform state within its chosen set. The marginal state law is stationary, hence equals $\pi$. Under any fixed word this mixture is still a mixture of uniform laws on $r_*$-sets. Its entropy is at least $\ln r_*$. For a word whose entire image has size $r_*$, all component sets map onto this same image, and the resulting entropy is exactly $\ln r_*$. If a minimum-rank word has length $L\ge1$ and iid occurrence probability $\alpha>0$, a window containing it also has minimum rank. Its absence in all disjoint $L$-blocks has probability at most $(1-\alpha)^{\lfloor k/L\rfloor}$. The residual entropy above $\ln r_*$ is therefore at most $(\ln m-\ln r_*)(1-\alpha)^{\lfloor k/L\rfloor}$. If the empty word already attains the minimum all supported maps are permutations and the residual is exactly zero. This characterizes how much of the stationary register can be learned from its finite input history, apart from learning the unknown maps or using the resulting state for a particular target. [Yano, *Random Walk in a Finite Directed Graph Subject to a Road Coloring*, §4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1005.0079v3#page=16)

The input partition remains a learning question. An exact limited case is a minimal two-state deterministic machine on $d$ uninterpreted letters with two distinct answer probabilities, queried from reset. At zero noise, inspect the empty word and every singleton. Reachability guarantees some letter $b$ takes the initial state to the other state; distinct current responses name the two states observationally. The $d$ words $ba$ now supply the missing image of the other state under each letter $a$. This learns which individual letters overwrite, retain or flip, with at most $2d+1$ endpoint labels and at most $3d$ raw letters. To see a matching order lower bound, fix one anchor letter that overwrites with one, initial state zero, and distinct binary readouts. The other $d-1$ maps are independently arbitrary among the four two-state maps. Distinct assignments yield different singleton or anchor-pair answers, so exact identification of a uniformly random assignment with constant confidence needs $\Omega(d)$ binary answers even if words are chosen adaptively. At fixed positive independent noise, known separated answer levels and replay permit a sufficient $O_\eta(d\log(d/\delta))$ endpoint reads by repeated calibration. Continuous text without resets or repeated endpoint queries has a different acquisition law. Two separate raw bits overwritten into the same one-bit register have identical erasure and age curves under fair iid input, yet only one predicts a target requesting that particular bit. Selecting the right raw vocabulary distinctions therefore needs the joint future-label law as well as information retention.

## Learning a one-bit update can encode a maximum cut on one token stream

**Proved for an explicit next-token model.** Let every raw symbol choose any of the four deterministic maps on a bit. From state $s$, assign probability $1/(2d)$ to each of $d$ vertex names and split the remaining half between ordinary tokens 0 and 1 using an independently fitted Bernoulli parameter $p_s$. Every forecast is normalized over all $d+2$ names. No transition is supplied, including those for 0 and 1. Exact maximum likelihood on one continuous sequence is NP-hard. For some fixed positive tolerance, approximating the optimum average loss to that additive tolerance is also NP-hard. This is a worst-case statement about finding the best table with these emission restrictions. It leaves the cost of a particular data distribution open.

**Edge comparisons.** Temporarily suppose observed 0 and 1 write their respective bits. For each oriented edge of a simple loopless graph, place the six ordinary tokens $uv1uv0$ on a stream starting this block in state zero. The two answer tokens test whether the composition $T_vT_u$ complements its starting bit. Their observed values also prepare the two starts, including the next block. Name-token losses are constant. Let $m$ be the edge count and $C$ the number of matched answers. If both endpoint updates are permutations, the edge contributes two matches precisely when one holds and the other flips. An edge involving a constant map contributes one. Independently replacing each constant vertex by a fair permutation gives an expected cut of $C/2$; the absence of self-loops matters. Hence $C^*=2\,\mathrm{MaxCut}(G)$, attained by a permutation table. Conditional expectation extracts a cut of size at least $C/2$ in polynomial time.

**Learning both heads.** Consider a simple cubic graph and $B$ calibration answers per state with positive fractions $1/4,3/4$. Use the label convention in which state one emits more ones on calibration. For $R$ reset vertices take a nonadjacent subset of size at least $|R|/4$ greedily. In the random rounding above, condition on the other vertices. Each chosen vertex has three neighbors, so choosing its better side improves expected cut by at least $1/2$. Therefore $\Delta=C^*-C\ge |R|/4$. At most $3|R|\le12\Delta$ edges involve a reset. Write the number of graph answers assigned to state one as $m+z$. Then $|z|\le\min(m,12\Delta)$ because permutation-only edges contribute one visit per state. In particular a maximum-score program has only permutations.

Put $n=B+m,\ u=B/2+C-m$ and $f(v)=\ln 2-h((1+v)/2)$ with binary entropy in nats. Fitting both unrestricted Bernoulli heads gains
\[
J(u,z)=F_u(n+z)+F_u(n-z),\qquad F_u(x)=x f(u/x)
\]
in log likelihood relative to fair binary answers. Indeed the graph has $m$ positive answers overall and $(C+z)/2$ of them reach state one. At $B\ge32m$, we have $(B/2-m)/(B+m)\ge15/33$ and $0\le u/x\le17/32$ for $x\in[n-m,n+m]$. Thus $F_u''(x)=(u/x)^2/[x(1-(u/x)^2)]\le1/B$. The advantage of imbalance over $z=0$ is at most $z^2/B\le(12/32)\Delta$. The balanced gain increases with $u$ at rate $2\operatorname{atanh}(u/n)\ge30/33$. Consequently every table is worse than a balanced optimum by at least $(47/88)\Delta$ nats after fitting the heads. Simple cubic Max-Cut is APX-hard. Alimonti and Kann establish maximum degree three in Theorem 4.2 and give the regular-degree extension just after the proof on p. 133. [Primary graph result](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0304-3975(98)00158-3)

**Learning the two apparent starts from the same stream.** Give the learner an initial observed token 0. Append $4m$ repetitions of the 32-symbol word $(1^30^3)^2(1^50^5)^2$, followed by the edge blocks. The two binary-token actions are among the parameters. Distinct constants give $B=64m$ calibration targets per state and the required fractions, up to exchanging state labels. Every other action pair has binary log loss at least $(2B-32)h(3/8)$ on calibration alone, even with free heads. To verify this, drop the first period. At the subsequent boundaries the bit returns to the same state. Two flips give row frequencies $3/8,5/8$. A single flip paired with identity gives $1/2$ in both rows. A flip paired with a constant gives $5/11,3/5$ up to state or symbol exchange. Identical constants, two identities, or a constant paired with identity leave one occupied row, with frequency $1/2$. Each of these cases has entropy at least $h(3/8)$. Distinct constants, coupled with any permutation table and fixed calibrated heads, cost at most $2B h(1/4)+2m\ln4$ in the variable part of the loss. For $m\ge6$ the wrong-calibration lower bound exceeds this upper bound by more than $m$ nats (already at six edges, and the difference is affine with slope greater than one). Thus an optimum learns the two synchronizing actions. A near-optimum below this gap does too. The $134m$ scored raw tokens yield a constant-scale transfer of a linear cut gap. Each vertex name is consumed exactly six times. Fixing total name probabilities is an explicit restriction on the predictive class; fully free name emissions would add a state-dependent term.

**Exact search with a different resource bound.** For a supplied chronological tape, fixed readout probabilities and initial state, let $w$ be the greatest number of raw names occurring on both sides of a cut. After a prefix retain the current bit and the actions assigned to its names that recur later. Introduce four choices on a first occurrence, apply the chosen update, score the relevant observation at its stated time, then drop a name at its last occurrence. Paths with the same retained state have identical possible futures on this tape. Max-product gives a globally best assignment; sum-product with independent priors charged once at introduction gives its exact weighted evidence. There are at most $2\cdot4^w$ retained entries and $O(N4^{w+1})$ scalar score operations for an $N$-position tape with indexed assignments. Recording witnesses or explicitly copying tuples requires additional storage or indexing work. For freely fitted binary heads, also track both positive and negative counts assigned to one state; prefix totals supply the other counts. This gives an additional $O((M+1)^2)$ factor for $M$ scored binary outcomes. A new continuation that reuses forgotten names needs their compatible posterior or a new pass. On the actual 262,144-word fit the peak was 9,466 simultaneous names.

**What the implemented learning rule pays.** Each raw name is optimized with all other actions fixed, recomputing its exact Dirichlet-integrated class likelihood under the candidate update. For a one-bit machine a changed state persists through a distinct hold or flip and disappears at the next distinct overwrite. On each occurrence of the edited name, apply the proposed map to the possibly changed predecessor bit. Scan affected class counts only up to the next occurrence or the first intervening overwrite. These disjoint intervals give exactly the same candidate count table as replaying the complete tape. In a literal two-bit suffix only the current and following positions can change. The general rule has finite ascent on a finite table with a strictly increasing objective; neither coordinate stationarity nor the eight-sweep limit certifies a global optimum. [Exact instance audits](action_cut.py) · [Implemented coordinate fits and held-out word scores](EVIDENCE.md#learning-which-raw-words-preserve-or-erase-a-bit)

## Three consecutive tokens identify a positive two-state source and its synchronization rate

**Proved under realizability.** A stationary source emits raw token $a$ from hidden bit $s$ with probability $p_s(a)>0$, then sets its bit to $T_a(s)$. Assume finite alphabet, distinct emission rows and positive stationary mass on both states. The tables of ordinary single, adjacent-pair and consecutive-triple probabilities determine every $T_a$, both complete emission rows and the stationary state distribution, up to a common exchange of hidden states. No reset words, access sequences, hidden labels, supplied word classes or interventions are required. The signed factor also determines an exact posterior-overlap decay rate. This unifies the earlier determinant reconstructions for synchronizing and purely permuting sources. [Earlier synchronizing case](#learning-a-synchronizing-bit-from-the-token-stream) · [Earlier permutation case](#recovering-a-continuing-update-from-three-token-windows)

Write $\mu_a=P(a)$, $K_{cb}=P(cb)-\mu_c\mu_b$ and $L^a_{cb}=\mu_aP(cab)-P(ca)P(ab)$. Give the maps hold, flip, and either write the signs $+1,-1,0$ respectively. Then $K$ has rank one and is nonzero. For **every** choice of letters,
\[
L^a_{cb}=r_a K_{cb},\qquad r_a=\operatorname{sign}(T_a)\,p_0(a)p_1(a).
\]
Using one nonzero entry of $K$, classify the sign of every $r_a$. For a zero coefficient, the entire row $P(\cdot\mid a)$ is the emission law of the state written by $a$. Group reset letters by equality of those rows. This identifies all four actions up to the hidden-state convention.

**Derivation.** Let $q$ be the stationary probability of state one, $q_c$ the posterior after observing $c$, and $d_b=p_1(b)-p_0(b)$. Then $K_{cb}=\mu_c(q_c-q)d_b$. If $K=0$, all singleton posteriors equal $q$. A reset cannot keep an interior belief fixed. On a hold letter, fixing $q$ requires equal emission probabilities. On every flip letter the ratio $p_1(a)/p_0(a)$ must equal $((1-q)/q)^2$. Normalization over the alphabet makes this common ratio one if there are any flip letters. This contradicts distinct emission rows. For a prior $x$, seeing $a$ first sends it to $x p_1(a)/\ell_a(x)$ where $\ell_a(x)=(1-x)p_0(a)+xp_1(a)$. The deterministic action next retains, complements or replaces that posterior. The difference between its values at $q_c$ and $q$ is $(q_c-q)r_a/[\ell_a(q_c)\ell_a(q)]$. Multiplication by $d_b$ converts this into $P(b\mid ca)-P(b\mid a)$. Clearing the positive denominators gives the displayed equality, including at entries where $K$ vanishes.

**Recovering emission parameters.** With both reset destinations represented, the two post-reset prediction rows give the emissions. With one reset destination, label it zero and take its row after a reset $v$. A flip $f$ must exist for state one to have positive stationary mass. Then $P(\cdot\mid vf)$ gives the other row. With no resets, select $(c,b)$ with $K_{cb}\ne0$ and write $w_j=K_{cj}/K_{cb}=d_j/d_b$. Both emission rows have the form $\mu+t_\pm w$ with $t_-<0<t_+$. For $w_j\ne0$,
\[
\frac{|r_j|-\mu_j^2}{w_j^2}=\frac{\mu_j}{w_j}(t_-+t_+)+t_-t_+ .
\]
The $w_j$ sum to zero and $w_b=1$, so two slopes of opposite sign determine the sum and product. The roots recover both rows. In every case $q=(\mu_b-p_0(b))/(p_1(b)-p_0(b))$ after fixing their order.

**What is sufficient for prediction when the initial bit is hidden?** The observer can instead keep the scalar $z=P(b\mid h)$, initialized at $\mu_b$. Let $\delta=P(b\mid c)-\mu_b$, $u_a=[P(a\mid c)-\mu_a]/\delta$ and $v_a=[P(ab\mid c)-P(ab)]/\delta$. At each raw token it predicts and then updates by
\[
P(a\mid h)=\mu_a+(z-\mu_b)u_a,\qquad
z'=\frac{P(ab)+(z-\mu_b)v_a}{\mu_a+(z-\mu_b)u_a}.
\]
Both unnormalized word probabilities are affine in the prior hidden-state probability. Interpolating between the empty history and $c$ proves the recursion at any subsequent history. This is a two-dimensional linear observable-operator realization after homogenizing the coordinate. Jaeger's general identification framework supplies the broader context. Here strict positivity and the two-state deterministic action constraint give a discrete classification as well as an observable predictor. Exact rational filtering can increase denominator length with time. A known current state needs one live bit and table lookups; an observer maintaining an uncertain initial state needs probability arithmetic or an explicit precision allowance. [Jaeger, *Observable Operator Models for Discrete Stochastic Time Series*](https://doi.org/10.1162/089976600300015411)

**From a local measurement to all time horizons.** Put $\rho=\sum_a\sqrt{|r_a|}$. If $Q_t$ is the posterior probability of the current state from $t$ tokens, starting at any prior $Q_0=x$, then
\[
\mathbb E_x\sqrt{Q_t(1-Q_t)}=\sqrt{x(1-x)}\,\rho^t .
\]
For one step, a reset contributes zero. On a permutation letter the posterior variance product under the square root is $x(1-x)p_0(a)p_1(a)/\ell_a(x)^2$. Weighting by $\ell_a(x)$ and summing proves the one-step identity for every posterior; iteration proves all horizons. Distinct normalized emission rows give $\rho\le\sum_a\sqrt{p_0(a)p_1(a)}<1$. The observer's expected state classification error is at most this overlap. Its excess next-token log loss over an observer who knows the current state is at most twice the overlap, since binary Shannon entropy is bounded by the order-one-half Rényi entropy $\ln(1+2\sqrt{x(1-x)})$. This is an exact rate for the displayed overlap observable. Other errors and entropy can have different rates. Earlier synchronization theorems prove exponential decay for broader finite-state sources. The square-root pair-transition weighting also occurs in the pairwise-merger construction of Riechers et al.; their target quantity includes quantum signal-state overlaps. The short-moment identification and the current-state posterior identity above follow from the displayed equations. [Travers and Crutchfield, §§VI–VII](https://arxiv.org/abs/1011.1581) · [Riechers et al., §IV](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevA.93.052317)

**Two observations can be insufficient even on two raw symbols.** Take $T_0=$ hold, $T_1=$ flip, $p_0(1)=3/8$, $p_1(1)=3/4$ and stationary $q=1/3$. The pair table is $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}9&7\\7&9\end{smallmatrix}\right)/32$. Exchanging the two observable symbol names preserves this table and gives raw symbol zero the flip action. A third source writes the observed bit and uses $p_0(1)=7/16,p_1(1)=9/16$. All three are positive, irreducible and aperiodic. Raw zero can hold, flip or write in sources with the very same entire adjacent-pair law. Their triple laws differ.

**Finite precision and acquisition.** Let $\gamma=\max_{c,b}|K_{cb}|$ and $e=\min_{s,a}p_s(a)$. With $d$ names and $J$ independent stationary triples, all needed empirical cells are within $u=\sqrt{\ln(2(d+d^2+d^3)/\delta)/(2J)}$ of their population values with probability at least $1-\delta$, using one consistent location per marginal length and Hoeffding's inequality. The resulting entry errors for $K,L^a$ are at most $3u,4u$. The maximal observed $|K|$ entry has true magnitude at least $\gamma-6u$. If $u\le\gamma e^2/32$, thresholding $|\widehat L^a|$ at $4u$ separates zeros from signed nonzeros at that entry. Reset destinations are grouped by $\mu_{a'}P(ab)-\mu_aP(a'b)$ at the same $b$. A distinct reset destination gives magnitude at least $e^2(\gamma-6u)$ since $|d_b|\ge |K_{cb}|$, while a shared destination gives zero. Its estimation error is at most $4u$. This provides an ordinary-data certificate under the stated margin; tiny margins or unseen words can preclude it. A two-pass implementation can find a witness with $O(d^2)$ pair counters, then accumulate its $O(d)$ selected slices on a replay. A literal one-pass dense triple table uses $O(d^3)$ counters. The finite-sample bound covers adaptive choice of witness through the simultaneous cell event.

The $\gamma^{-2}$ sample exponent cannot generally improve at a fixed positive emission floor. Give four names emission rows $(1/4+\xi,1/4-\xi,1/4,1/4)$ and its first-two-coordinate exchange, with $0<\xi\le1/8$. Hold on the first two, flip on the third, compare hold versus flip on the fourth. Both sources have stationary mass $1/2$ per state and identical pair laws with $\gamma=\xi^2$. Only four triple probabilities change, each by $\xi^2/2$. All cells exceed or equal $1/512$, so the one-sample KL divergence is $O(\xi^4)$ by the chi-square upper bound. Testing the two sources with constant confidence on independent stationary triples requires $\Omega(\xi^{-4})=\Omega(\gamma^{-2})$ observations by product KL and Pinsker's inequality. This lower bound concerns the triple-sampling interface. A continuous long trajectory can carry additional long-range evidence.

On one stationary irreducible trajectory, ergodic frequency estimates converge to the population cylinders. For an aperiodic two-state chain, let $\beta=|1-P_{01}-P_{10}|<1$. Windows of three observed tokens separated by $g$ discarded steps can be coupled to $J$ independent stationary windows with probability of disagreement at most $(J-1)\beta^g$, since the total-variation distance between the two possible hidden start laws contracts by $\beta^g$. Periodic alternation still has ergodic time averages but this bound does not decay. In the all-holds case both states are absorbing, so one path samples only one emission row of a stationary mixture. If the rows coincide, arbitrary update tables generate the same iid text. These distinctions separate a population identification theorem from pathwise estimation and from agnostic fitting on natural language. [Rational checks and finite passive fits](EVIDENCE.md#learning-a-hidden-bit-from-ordinary-token-triples)

## Recovering a finite hidden machine when predictive dimension is smaller than state count

**Proved for exact word laws.** A stationary source has $k$ hidden states. In state $i$ the probability of each raw letter $a$ is $p_i(a)>0$, and seeing it deterministically sets the state to $T_a(i)$. Assume the transition graph is strongly connected and every two states differ on the probability of some future word. Write $n=\operatorname{rank}(P(uv))_{u,v}$ for the real stationary word Hankel rank. Given $n$, ordinary word probabilities through length $2n-1$ determine a terminating reconstruction of **all $k$ states, their emissions, their raw-letter maps and their stationary masses**, up to renaming. The learner discovers $k$ during reconstruction, including when $k>n$. The construction uses exact rank, subspace and spectral decisions.

Finite history/generator equivalence already establishes intrinsic predictive states from the full stationary process. Reconstruction of linear observable operators from a Hankel basis is also established. The result below identifies the physical state rows inside the linear realization, including linearly dependent rows, using minimum images of the learned operators. [Travers and Crutchfield, Theorem 1 and Corollary 1](https://www.mdpi.com/2073-8994/17/1/78) · [Jaeger, corrected author edition](https://www.ai.rug.nl/minds/uploads/oom_neco00.pdf)

**Observable operators.** In a rank-$n$ word law, prefix rows and suffix columns through length $n-1$ span the respective spaces: they start in one dimension, grow by letter shifts and a step without growth is invariant under further shifts. Choose $n$ prefixes $U$ and $n$ suffixes $V$ with $H=(P(uv))_{u\in U,v\in V}$ invertible. Form $B_a=H^{-1}(P(uav))_{u,v}$, terminal column $\tau=H^{-1}(P(u))_u$ and initial row $\alpha=(P(v))_v$. Right extension sends a predictive row $x$ to $xB_a$, and $x\tau$ gives its mass. This holds first on the independent prefix rows, then by linearity since extension preserves relations of full future laws. The largest measured word length is $2n-1$. The rank promise or a certified finite realization is an input. An observed nonzero minor only supplies a rank lower bound.

**Separating states on a minimum image.** In the physical machine take a word of minimum image cardinality $r$, with image $A$. Every word maps $A$ injectively to another minimum image. All minimum images communicate: a word with full image $B$ sends $A$ into $B$ with the same cardinality. Order $A$ once and consider the finite graph of ordered images $\phi$ reached by words. It is strongly connected. A path back to the set $A$ gives a permutation there; repeating that return composition finitely many times restores its ordering.

For $i\ne j$ in $A$, some word returning $A$ **pointwise** has $P_i(w)\ne P_j(w)$. Otherwise append a common return to any two words reaching the same ordered image $\phi$. Their probability ratios from $i$ and $j$ must agree, defining $h(\phi)$. One more letter gives
\[
h(T_a\phi)/h(\phi)=p_{\phi(i)}(a)/p_{\phi(j)}(a),\qquad
h(\phi)=\sum_a p_{\phi(j)}(a) h(T_a\phi).
\]
The maximum principle on this finite strongly connected graph with positive edge weights makes $h$ constant. Its empty-word value is one. Then all future word laws from $i,j$ coincide, a contradiction. If the ordered-image graph has $M$ vertices, some distinguishing pointwise loop has length at most $2M-1$. Choose shortest outgoing and return representatives of length at most $M-1$. Equality of all pointwise loop probabilities through $2M-1$ suffices to compare each outgoing representative plus one letter against the next representative by the same return, proving the impossible harmonic equation.

**Why dependent predictive rows can still be found.** Each pointwise return is diagonal on $A$ with entries equal to its word probabilities. These diagonals separate coordinates and their real algebra contains each coordinate projection. For a chosen $i$, for each $j\ne i$ subtract the $j$ entry from a separating diagonal, divide by the difference at $i$, and multiply the resulting diagonals. Products are linear combinations of actual pointwise return words. Applying these projections to the all-ones terminal column proves independence of the future laws on $A$. Conversely, the stationary state row after a minimum word has a positive coefficient at every member of $A$. Applying the projections to this unnormalized row puts each corresponding physical coordinate row in the span of ordinary unnormalized prefix rows. Forward transport and strong connectivity put every physical coordinate row in that span. Thus each state's future law has a normalized observable row $v_i$, with $v_i\tau=1$ and $v_iB_a=p_i(a)v_{T_a(i)}$. Linear relations among these rows are exactly those among their full future laws.

No state outside $A$ lies in the span of the rows of $A$. Suppose $v_s=\sum_{i\in A}c_i v_i$ has nonzero coefficients at $i,j$. Choose a pointwise return $w$ separating them. In the normalized row $v_{T_{w^t}(s)}$ the ratio of these coefficients is $(c_i/c_j)(P_i(w)/P_j(w))^t$. It takes infinitely many values. Independence on $A$ gives unique coefficients and the source has only finitely many states. A single nonzero coefficient must instead equal one by normalization, identifying a member of $A$ by predictive distinguishability.

**Constructing the graph.** Start with the whole observable row space. Compute $UB_a$ for each discovered subspace $U$ and letter and deduplicate by exact row-space equality. Each is the span of the physical rows in an actual word image, so the search terminates after at most $2^k-1$ spaces without knowing $k$. Every image $S$ contains a minimum image (prepend a minimum word to the word reaching $S$). The preceding no-other-state property implies that the smallest observed dimension is $r$ and the minimum-dimensional spaces correspond one-to-one to physical minimum sets. This also handles updates that merge some states while preserving others forever.

Choose one such space $U_A$. Enumerate products returning $U_AB_w=U_A$. Each acts there as a positive weighted permutation in the hidden row basis. Its spectrum is entirely positive real exactly when its permutation fixes $A$ pointwise: a cycle of length $l$ contributes its positive geometric-mean weight times all $l$-th roots of unity. Refine simultaneous **row** eigenspaces of the positive-spectrum returns until all $r$ are lines. Pairwise separation proves termination. Normalize each line by $v\tau=1$. From any discovered row and letter compute its emission probability $vB_a\tau$ and its successor $vB_a/(vB_a\tau)$. Add each new successor and continue until closure. Strong connectivity yields all $k$ states; the recovered stochastic transition graph yields its unique stationary masses. This works even when the stationary observable row admits several linear decompositions. Long operator products used by this search require arithmetic on the learned matrices and no further raw observations.

A synchronized table executor needs a live index of $\lceil\log_2 k\rceil$ bits and an implementation of the updates and emissions. An uncertain observer can propagate an $n$-coordinate predictive row and must account for probability precision. Writing down the full physical graph costs at least $k$ rows. Finite measurement precision and statistical stability need their own analysis, even with fixed $n$ and uniformly positive emissions. [Exact finite reconstructions and period-resolution evidence](EVIDENCE.md#recovering-more-hidden-states-than-predictive-coordinates)

**Finding the same lines without enumerating long return words.** For every minimum subspace $U_B$, choose an observable path isomorphism $J_B$ from a common base $U_A$ to $U_B$, taking $J_A=I$. In row coordinates an edge $a:B\to C$ gives the known matrix $G_{B,a}=J_B(B_a|_{U_B\to U_C})J_C^{-1}$ on the base. These matrices are all positive weighted permutations in the same intrinsic basis on $A$. Products along a return path telescope to its original action. Hence their products include diagonal words separating every pair, as above.

For each generator $G$ form the linear map $C_G:X\mapsto GXG^{-1}$ on $r$-by-$r$ matrices and generate the unital algebra $\mathcal A=\operatorname{span}\{C_{G_w}\}$. A word basis is obtained by extending independent basis words by each generator until the span stops growing. Its dimension is at most $r^4$ and basis-word length is at most $r^4-1$. In the unknown diagonal matrix algebra $D$, conjugation merely permutes the diagonal entries. Its trace is accessible directly from the spectrum of $G_w$:
\[
\chi(w)=\sum_{\lambda\in\operatorname{spec}(G_w)}\frac{\lambda}{|\lambda|}
       =\#\{\text{fixed points of the underlying permutation}\}.
\]
Eigenvalues are counted with multiplicity. All phases of each cycle of length greater than one sum to zero. This defines a linear functional on $\mathcal A$ because it is precisely the trace of the restriction to $D$. Compute the nullspace of the bilinear form $(X,Y)\mapsto\chi(XY)$ using products of basis words. This nullspace $I$ consists exactly of the elements acting as zero on $D$. Indeed the restricted algebra is spanned by permutations and contains their transposes (each inverse is a positive power). For a nonzero restriction one can pair with its transpose to obtain a positive sum of squares.

Now $D=\bigcap_{X\in I}\ker X$. One inclusion follows immediately. For the other, every pointwise return contributes $C_{G_w}-I_{\rm op}$ to the nullspace; applied to a hidden off-diagonal matrix unit $E_{ij}$ it multiplies it by $P_i(w)/P_j(w)-1$. Some return separates every such pair, so only diagonal matrices survive all the kernels. Common eigenlines of the resulting $D$ give the desired hidden lines on $U_A$. This uses polynomially many exact linear algebra and eigenvalue operations in $r$, the alphabet size and the number of discovered subspaces. It does not give a polynomial bit-precision or statistical bound. When every letter permutes the states there is just one image subspace. The construction also covers intermediate image ranks without assuming any raw word synchronizes the entire machine.

**The cost of discovering how many states there are.** Return to the [three-symbol marked probability clock](#exact-and-attainable-prediction-on-a-probability-clock). At age $j$ it ticks with probability $t\in(0,1)$ and otherwise resets with one of two colors. The conditional red probability is $h_k(j)=(1+c\cos(2\pi j/k))/2$ with fixed $0<c<1$ and integer $k\ge3$. Ages modulo $k$ give exactly $k$ distinct discrete predictive states. After a reset followed by $j$ ticks the phase is known. The present and the next tick's color probabilities together separate any two phases since $\sin(2\pi/k)\ne0$. All states and all three raw emissions have the reachability and positivity required above. The earlier clock calculation gives Hankel rank three for every $k$. Thus even the three-symbol alphabet, rank and positivity floor can stay fixed while the physical reconstruction has arbitrarily many rows.

For stationary geometric age $J$ with $\Pr(J=j)=(1-t)t^j$, the relative entropy rate from the period-$k$ law to the period-$(k+1)$ law is
\[
D_{\rm adj}=(1-t)\mathbb E\,\mathrm{KL}(\mathrm{Bern}(h_k(J))\Vert\mathrm{Bern}(h_{k+1}(J))).
\]
The observed last reset fixes the age under either hypothesis almost surely. A finite segment started with its stationary age hidden has KL at most its length times this rate, by revealing the common initial-age draw and applying the chain rule. The mean value theorem and $|\sin x|\le |x|$ give $|h_k(j)-h_{k+1}(j)|=O(j^2/k^3)$. At $j=1$ its magnitude is $\Theta(k^{-3})$. The two mark probabilities stay uniformly inside $(0,1)$, so Bernoulli KL is bounded above and below by constant multiples of squared difference. Therefore $D_{\rm adj}=\Theta(k^{-6})$. The best iid raw-word model uses the same tick rate and red fraction $\bar h_k=\mathbb E h_k(J)$. Its excess rate is $\Theta(k^{-4})$: typical mark deviations across ages are of order $k^{-2}$, with a geometric fourth-moment bound above and positive weight on ages zero and one below.

Distinguishing the two given adjacent periods with both errors at most $\delta\le1/8$ requires and suffices to observe $\Theta_{t,c}(k^6\ln(1/\delta))$ consecutive raw tokens. The KL testing lower bound follows by applying binary data processing to the decision. For the upper bound divide the stream into disjoint triples. Retain those with a reset of either color, then a tick, then another reset. Their selection indicators depend only on independent tick coins, with constant probability $(1-t)^2t$. The last mark has an independent Bernoulli law with mean $h_k(1)$ or $h_{k+1}(1)$. A count bound and Hoeffding's inequality separate the two means at the stated scale. This is a simple-hypothesis discrimination result. At the fixed parameters $t=c=1/2$, a Taylor expansion at $h_0=3/4$, dominated by the geometric fourth moment, gives
\[
\lim k^6D_{\rm adj}=100\pi^4,\qquad
\lim k^4D_{\rm iid}=22\pi^4.
\]
Here $\mathbb E J^2=3$, $\mathbb E J^4=75$. The adjacent color difference has leading term $-2c\pi^2j^2/k^3$. The deviations from the fitted iid color mean have leading term $-c\pi^2(j^2-\mathbb E J^2)/k^2$. These rates quantify the gap between gaining predictive accuracy and identifying an exact period. Thus the probabilities of words of length at most five identify each finite clock under the rank promise. The precision and number of raw observations needed to distinguish neighbors grow with the period. The earlier projection/rotation probability clock and its correction are due to Jaeger. [Author's 2012 addendum](https://www.ai.rug.nl/minds/uploads/addendumProbClock.pdf) · [Numerical rates and exact reconstructions](EVIDENCE.md#recovering-more-hidden-states-than-predictive-coordinates)
